  the check's projection > 225 GiB) replaces L-pq-ps050-s1 with L-pq-s1 (x0.25, runs/fallback.txt) and resumes the
  queue; reason avail (machine < 30 GiB available) pauses the queue. Tested in a /tmp sandbox with fake shadow processes.

  and the PQ mesh size per joined PQ topic by class.
- Report rule: a 1 Gbit-class shortfall seen only at x0.25 is unconfirmed.
- Pending (Yann via coordinator): cells after L stay 2,048 x 6 at x0.25, or switch to 1,024 x 20 at the highest peer scale
  that fits (~x0.5). Without an answer before L finishes, keep the plan.
- x0.25 mesh thinning with PQ load, stopped R-pq-s1 slots 0–4 (indicative): mesh links per node 222 → 168 → 144 → 155 → 128;

## Memory fixes from the orb (realism-fix1.patch, sha256 60cc235a…), approved by the coordinator 14:2x Nov 7
- Fix 1, lazy gossipsub queue: libp2p-gossipsub 0.49.5 allocates each peer's forward/IHAVE channel bounded(2,500) up front
  (~0.55 MiB per connection). The patch (sim/gossipsub-0.49.5-lazy-queue.patch, built from sim/wbsim/gossipsub via
  [patch.crates-io]) makes it unbounded with the same 2,500 cap checked in send_message. Reviewed: the only other use of
  the sender is .len(). Orb A/B (64 hosts, model_unblocked_syscall_latency false): byte-identical event logs; with it on
  (our runs) traces diverge like a seed change.
- Fix 2, sched.json: gen_config writes the PQ schedule to <run>/sched.json and leaves run.json "sched": [] for pub_dir runs
  (every realism run); analyze.py/realism_check.py read it through analyze.read_cfg. Checked here: patched analysis on the
  old-format run P512-ps100 gives byte-identical realism.json, summary2.json and levers.md. pqsum.py does not read sched.
- Binaries: bin/old/wbsim (sha256 d9d4fc55…, = repo/sim/wbsim/target/release/wbsim, the default path; every run before
  P512-ps100-fx-s1) and bin/fixed/wbsim (sha256 3d2f6765…, lazy queue). Runs with -fx in their name use --binary bin/fixed/wbsim (named wbsim so comm, memlog/watchdog and the wbsim.*.stdout globs keep working).
- Orb diagnosis of failed forwards without PQ: the 1,024 aggregates per slot (495 B, under the 1,000 B IDONTWANT threshold)
  forwarded to D-1 mesh peers within ~1 s are 2-4 MB, more than a 25/15 Mbit/s uplink sends before the 1 s forward-queue
  timeout; Lighthouse keeps rust-libp2p's defaults (forward 1 s, publish 5 s, 5,000 per queue), so real clients drop them
  too. 95 % logged 10-12 s into the slot. Loss matters on 1 Gbit hosts (Shadow uses Reno on 200 ms paths). No change.
- Calibration report v2 from the orb copied to repo/results/realism-calibration.md (my 2,048 x 20 numbers checked).

## CPU finding 14:47 Nov 7: wbsim deep-copies its whole RunCfg on every action
- main.rs act() starts with `let cfg = self.cfg.clone();` and runs for every Action, including Validated (each received
  message after verification), Pub, Tick, PeerManager. RunCfg holds all nodes (validators lists, topics, dial lists,
  offline windows) and, before realism-fix1, the whole PQ schedule (Vec<Vec<Vec<u32>>>, ~100k small vectors at 50 slots).
- Explains the wall times: R-pq-s1 (50-slot schedule, run.json 4.3 MB) 43 min per slot and 12 min of warm-up vs R6-pq-s1
  (6-slot schedule, 1.5 MB) 11 min per slot and 4 min of warm-up, same hosts, seed and load. Linear fit: ~6.6 min per slot
  without the schedule copy, + ~0.36 min per slot per 1,000 schedule entries.
- sched.json (realism-fix1) empties sched in run.json, so the schedule copy is gone for every new run (L run.json 572 KB).
  The node-list copy remains (~1.1 MB of JSON at 2,048 hosts); Arc<RunCfg> would remove it. Simulated behaviour does not
  depend on it (Shadow does not model user-space CPU time), apart from seed-like divergence via the syscall-latency model.

## Nov 07 19:2x-23:3x: L analysed, PSS accounting, coordinator items 1-4, four 2,048 x 6 x1.0 cells done
(19:31 and 21:32 scheduled checks and the 20:12 milestone message were missed: agent rate limit; the queue ran unattended.)
- New tools: .amp-out/pqdiag.py RUN [--slots A B] (item 1: per-class node-slot completeness, PQ message miss rate at 8 s
  and 9 s, incomplete node-slots by first matching reason (rejoined <= 12 s / 12-60 s, whole subnet missing, never
  delivered, near miss <= 1 s, late > 1 s), repeat hosts, worst hosts; BLS attestation miss rate at 8 s / never per class
  and subscription type; item 2: per-slot mesh links, PQ mesh degree per class, negative-score peers by observer and by
  scored-peer class, score bands, penalty counters, BadScore churn and timed-out forwards per topic group, negatives vs
  the observer's failed sends). runs/memsum.py (summed RSS and PSS). .amp-out/scratch/bgexcess.py (first deliveries per
  slot window per topic group / run median: > 1 = old messages accepted again).
- Item 3 (19:39, R6-pq-ps100-fx-s1 sim 58): summed RSS 139.3 GiB, summed PSS 125.5, MemAvailable drop 127.0 (idle 495).
  RSS - PSS = wbsim Shared_Clean 6.9 MiB per process (the binary). watchdog256.sh and memlog.sh switched to PSS 19:41
  (cap still 235 GiB PSS; memlog adds pss= and projpss=).
- Score driver (item 2): by elimination the slow-peer penalty (rust-libp2p 0.49: -10 per failed send to that peer, decay
  0.1 per 12 s, threshold 0; applied to every client in the sim). P3 deficits 0 until slot 15, then ~500 per slot
  network-wide; P5 (IP colocation) 0; P4 unused; P7 inputs ~600 GraftBackoff + ~40 BrokenPromise per slot (L). Negatives
  per observer rise with the observer's own failed sends in the previous 12 s (L: 0 -> 4.9, 1-99 -> 9.1, 100-999 -> 11.8,
  1k-5k -> 19.5, >5k -> 29.7). Timed-out forwards per slot (L, summed): aggregates 450-1,040k, PQ 250-660k, attestations
  180-740k, blocks/columns ~0; queue-full refusals (cloud only) are not split by topic.
- Recirculation artifact (L from slot 28; R-nopq-ps100-s1 att +3-13 % in slots 12-19; none in any 6-slot cell): wbsim's
  60 s duplicate cache (Lighthouse ~397 s) and no age check let a message held > 60 s (stalled TCP, RTO backoff) re-flood
  the whole network: L blocks exactly 2.0x first deliveries in slots 40-41, PQ +2-25 % in 17 of 22 slots from slot 28,
  aggregates up to 2.1x. Fix: --max-msg-age 60 (perturb.py -> run.json max_msg_age_s; wbsim IGNOREs a flood message
  received > 60 s after publish, counts stale_pq / stale_bg in ticks). Binary bin/stale/wbsim (sha256 6505d446...,
  = bin/fixed source + this check only; built 23:23 in build-target-stale). Used for the long key cell.

### L-pq-ps100-fx-s1 (1,024 x 50, x1.0, fixed) results — wall 15,445 s, peak RSS 131.3 GiB (~124 GiB PSS)
- Completeness slots 2-49: 50/15 99.95 % (worst slot 99.37), 50/25 99.99 % (worst 99.6), cloud 98.15 % (worst 96.72;
  ~98.8 % in slots 2-16, ~97.8 % in 17-49). Pass rule FAIL (cloud only).
- PQ message miss at 8 s: 0.018 / 0.0004 / 0.29 %; at 9 s 0.012 / 0.0003 / 0.26 %. BLS att miss at 8 s same run:
  0.24 / 0.12 / 3.5 % (cloud 2sub 0.53, all64 3.9).
- Incomplete node-slots: 4 / 3 / 325. Cloud: whole subnet 62 (51k missing msgs), never 163 (46k), near miss 51, late 49;
  none after a restart/join. 23 of 366 cloud hosts, the same ones slot after slot (worst 47 of 48 slots): far-region
  (syd, gru, sel, sin, tyo) mostly Prysm (target 70), 30-55 of ~72 peers negative; 193 of 210 whole-subnet misses had an
  empty mesh on that subnet with 20-30 connected subscribers, 60-90 % of them negative. No message missed by > 50 %.

## Nov 08 00:0x: client-faithful scoring (coordinator, after a per-client source review)
- Coordinator's review: rust-libp2p clients (Lighthouse v8.3.2 on the sigp fork, gossipsub 0.51; Grandine) penalise slow
  peers exactly as the sim does (+1 per failed message incl. control, weight -10, threshold 0, decay 0.1 per 12 s,
  decay_to_zero 0.01; a peer below 0 is pruned from every mesh with a 60 s backoff). Prysm (go-libp2p-pubsub v0.18.0):
  no term, a full 1,000-RPC queue only drops and counts. Nimbus: term with weight 0.0. Lodestar: none. Teku: +1 to P7
  only after >= 4 MiB pending for 3 heartbeats (P7 threshold 6), may never fire on TCP + mplex -> treated as 0. None of
  the four non-Rust libraries expires queued messages (no 1 s forward / 5 s publish timeout): caveat, unchanged here.
- Change: GsCfg.slow_peer_weight (per node, overrides scoring.slow_peer_weight in build_swarm); perturb.py
  --client-slow-peer sets it to 0.0 on prysm/teku/nimbus/lodestar hosts. 'other' (Hermes, Erigon/Caplin, Grandine,
  unknown) is not split by client and keeps -10. Binary bin/cf/wbsim (sha256 248f7d69..., = bin/stale source + this),
  built in build-target-cf. No other change.
- Validation T64-cf (64 x 3, x1.0, seed 1, --max-msg-age 60 --client-slow-peer) vs T64-age60-stale (same seed, every
  client -10), .amp-out/cfcheck.py: negative-score peers per observer per slot (own failed sends per 12 s, lowest score):
  stale: lighthouse 6.67, lodestar 10.0, nimbus 7.25, other 8.86, prysm 9.07, teku 9.88 (lowest -5k to -70k);
  cf: lighthouse 6.03 (-57k), other 7.07 (-5k), lodestar 0.00, nimbus 0.00, teku 0.00 (0.0; 830-5,080 own failed sends
  per 12 s), prysm 0.03 (one peer at -253 = P7 behaviour penalty, (10 - 6)^2 x -15.9). PASS. Node configs identical
  apart from slow_peer_weight (28 hosts at 0.0).
- Queue head (00:0x): R20-pq-ps100-cf-s1 (2,048 x 20), R6-nopq-ps100-cf-s1, R6-pq-fs0-ps100-cf-s1, R6-pq-b16-ps100-cf-s1,
  then R6-pq-s1k-ps100-fx-s1 and R20-pq-ps100-age60-s1 (conservative). R6-pq-v400-ps100-fx-s1 kept running (started 23:45).

### Item 3 analysis (.amp-out/failsplit.py; all runs x1.0, fixed binary, every client -10)
- Chronic cloud hosts = incomplete in >= half of the analysed slots (cloud hosts by client in the key cell: lighthouse 401,
  prysm 141, nimbus 63, teku 59, other 53, lodestar 25):
  key cell R6-pq-ps100-fx-s1: 12 = 9 prysm (target 70: syd 640, gru 1873, gru 988, sel 1972, sin 344, gru 852, sel 440,
  sin 346, syd 1520) + 3 teku (target 100: sin 1639, syd 493, sin 455). Incomplete at least once: lighthouse 2, prysm 10, teku 3.
  seed 2: 7 prysm (gru, syd, sel, ...); at least once: lighthouse 1, other 1, prysm 15.
  fs0: 12 = 7 prysm + 3 teku + 2 lighthouse (988 gru, 930 syd; 2 of 4 slots each); at least once: lighthouse 7, prysm 14, teku 3.
  L (48 slots): 6 = 5 prysm (syd 640 47/48, syd 273 41/48, gru 435 40/48, sel 308 37/48, sin 538 26/48) + 1 teku (syd 289
  27/48); >= 2 slots: lighthouse 7 (max 14/48), other 1 (20/48), prysm 11, teku 1.
  -> No 'other' (Grandine) host is chronic anywhere; Lighthouse only in fs0 (2 hosts, 2/4 slots).
- Failed sends that cost slow-peer score, share by kind (whole run): key cell fwd timeout 54.3 %, fwd queue-full 19.3 %,
  IHAVE/IWANT/IDONTWANT queue-full 26.3 %, own publish 0.0 % (timeouts and refusals); seed 2 53.4 / 18.6 / 28.0 / 0;
  fs0 63.0 / 23.8 / 13.2 / 0; L 60.4 / 16.3 / 23.2 / 0. Home hosts: 100 % forward timeouts (their queues never fill).
  Cloud hosts: control queue-full 32-41 % (fs0 20-22 %), fwd queue-full 22-27 % (fs0 34-42 %), fwd timeout 35-47 %.
  IDONTWANT is not separable from IHAVE/IWANT with the logged counters, so 26 % (key cell) / 32-41 % (cloud) is an
  upper bound for its share. Lighthouse's fork keeps IDONTWANT in a separate 20,000-slot control queue and all data in
  one 5,000-slot queue, so 0.49.5 over-penalises Rust hosts by up to that share (control) plus part of the forward
  queue-full share. Queues unchanged.
- Publish-counter leak (code: Receiver::poll_stale pops a stale Publish from the priority stream without the
  priority_queue_len.fetch_sub that Receiver::poll_next does, so each own publish dropped after 5 s keeps one of the
  peer's 2,500 publish slots until that peer disconnects): in L, 46.5k stale own publishes over 50 slots (0.1-2.6k per
  slot, 9.4k in slots 0-9, 6.3k in 40-49) but only 9.5k own-publish refusals in total, sporadic (slots 16, 19-20, 23,
  26-28: 0.4-2.8k each), 0.0k in slots 0-15 and ~0.1k in slots 29-49; per connection 0.000-0.012 per slot. No growth
  over time -> latent here (connection churn, conn_life 1,800 s, resets counters). Not patched.

- Queue: R20-pq-b16-ps100-cf-s1 appended after R6-pq-noloss-ps100-cf-s1 (bin/cf, nofallback marker).
- PQ topics' P3 (eth_peer_score): topic weight 1/16; expected rate = msgs/16 per subnet per slot (125 / 93.75 / 62.5 at 800k / 600k / 400k); mesh decay 0.9647 per slot (128-slot decay time); threshold = (rate/50)·d/(1−d) = 68.2 / 51.2 / 34.1; cap 16 × threshold; activation 17 slots (204 s, as attestation subnets); window 2 s; P3 and P3b weight −1/16, so a full deficit costs −threshold²/256 = −18.2 / −10.2 / −4.5 per PQ topic (Lighthouse formula, every host in bin/cf). Blocks and aggregates activate after 1 epoch (384 s), i.e. never within a 20-slot run (stop 280 s).
- b16 rate fix (coordinator: fix it): perturb.py sets the PQ expected rate from the mean number of messages actually published per slot when --batch or --bundler is on (unbatched cells unchanged: msgs/16). Check on the R20-pq-b16-ps100-cf-s1 config (generated with the queue line's flags into scratch, then deleted): pq_counts mean 1,070.85 per slot → rate 66.93, threshold 36.54, cap 584.6, full-deficit cost −5.22 per PQ topic (was 68.2 / −18.2 with rate 125). 6-slot b16 cells are unaffected (P3 never activates in 6 slots).
- P3 on R20-pq-ps100-cf-s1 (.amp-out/p3split.py → p3split-R20-pq-ps100-cf-s1.txt):
  - MessageDeficit penalties (counted per peer, topic and heartbeat) are 0 up to slot 14, then per slot 15-19 by observer client: Lighthouse (1,115 hosts) 347 / 675 / 638 / 606 / 552; Prysm (397) 1,753 / 687 / 672 / 240 / 260; Nimbus (178) 686 / 220 / 196 / 209 / 209; Teku (156) 442 / 272 / 330 / 204 / 185; Lodestar (58) 222 / 119 / 81 / 39 / 58; other (144) 83 / 136 / 99 / 139 / 166. Per host, Prysm and Nimbus views see ~10× more deficits at activation than Lighthouse views (D = 8 vs Lighthouse's 5/3/10 meshes).
  - By topic: bin/cf has no per-topic deficit counter. Blocks and aggregates cannot contribute (activation 384 s > 280 s), so these are attestation-subnet and PQ-subnet deficits. cf2 adds message_deficit_per_topic.
  - Network-wide, P3 is small next to the slow-peer term: ~3.5k deficit penalties at slot 15 and ~2k after are ~150-300 peer-topic pairs at a time (≈12-17 heartbeats per slot), against ~16-17k negative-score peers per slot that do not step up at slot 15/16 (16,772 / 17,210 / 16,585 / 16,482 / 16,555 / 15,972 / 16,672 in slots 13-19). PQ BadScore churn is also flat (5.5-7.7k per slot before and after).
  - The 8 chronic hosts, mean per slot over slots 2-14 → 15-19: PQ Prune received 640 35 → 25, 1520 44 → 26, 1422 38 → 11, 803 42 → 27, 1811 24 → 38, 852 22 → 29, 1972 32 → 30, 1518 (Teku) 17 → 51. Views scoring them negative stay at 12-45 throughout; only 1518 (15 → 25-27) and 1811 (up to 27) rise after slot 15. Own MessageDeficit penalties: 1518 81 / 81 / 29 / 12 / 0, 1811 18 / 6 / 28 / 0 / 0, the others 0-2. 1518's in-degree in others' PQ meshes falls from 105-141 to 71-84 in slots 16-18.
  - The slot-16 step (cloud 97.57 % in slots 16, 18, 19; pqdiag-R20-pq-ps100-cf-s1-s16-19.txt): 67 incomplete cloud node·slots on 27 hosts in slots 16-19, by reason never 27 (4,824 msgs), late > 1 s 27 (2,124), near miss 9, whole subnet 4. Verdict: P3 is not the driver; the step adds more far-region Prysm (70-peer) cloud hosts.

- Nov 08 14:4x coordinator: (1) Shadow 3.3's TCP is not "Reno without SACK": SACK recovery (tcp.c, e.g. L1850 _tcp_removeSacks, L1899; checked here), RFC 6298 RTO with 200 ms min / 1 s initial (definitions.h L46-L48; checked), IW10; vs Linux: Reno not CUBIC, no TLP/RACK (slower), cwnd 10 after RTO instead of 1, no slow-start restart after idle (faster). Report caveat and verdict corrected; the "Reno without SACK" wording above is wrong. (2) R20-pq-noloss-ps100-cf2-s1 replaced in place by R6-nopq-noloss-ps100-cf-s1 (2,048 x 6, --no-pq --loss-scale 0, bin/cf; matched pair with R6-nopq-ps100-cf-s1); nofallback marker moved to it. ### Coordinator 21:2x Nov 9: lnxbuf (Linux-default socket buffers) — not queued (21:50)
- Realistic runs use Shadow's default autotuning: no socket_* options in shadow.yaml; libp2p-tcp 0.44.1 and wbsim set no SO_SNDBUF/SO_RCVBUF.
- Built shadow-src-lnxbuf (shadow-src + repo/sim/shadow-3.3-lnxbuf.patch: _tcp_computeMax[WR]MEM return 4/6 MiB, non-loopback sockets skip the bandwidth x delay initial sizing, LNXBUF_LOG-gated info logs) -> shadow-install-lnxbuf/bin/shadow. A clean build is needed after editing tcp.c (incremental ./setup build compiles but the install kept the old binary).
- Finding: no socket ever gets an RTT sample. The timestamp echo is set in tcp_networkInterfaceIsAboutToSendPacket from tcp->receive.lastTimestamp, which _tcp_processPacket clears after _tcp_flush (tcp.c L2441), so every packet has echo 0 and L2364 never calls _tcp_updateRTTEstimate. Instrumented T64-lnxbuf (64 hosts, 3 slots, realistic flags, 2.4 kB): 0 first-RTT samples over the full run; the first 62 logged packets all echo 0. Consequences: (1) _tcp_tuneInitialBufferSizes never runs -> buffers start at 131,072 / 174,760; (2) _tcp_computeRTTMEM uses rttSmoothed = 0 -> caps CLAMP(0, 4 MiB, 40 MiB) = 4 MiB and 6 MiB already; (3) receive autotune never resets per RTT (2 x cumulative bytes); (4) RTO = 1 s from creation (L2948), x2 per timeout (L1540), restored to 1 s only after > 2 backoffs (L2011-L2016): sticky 2 s / 4 s RTO after one or two timeouts. Linux ~RTT + 200 ms.
- lnxbufcheck (final binary, full T64): no socket above 131,072 / 174,760 initially (no initial events at all), send max 4,194,304 (819 steps at the cap), receive max 6,291,456: PASS. T64 with the original binary: all 64 hosts' wbsim stdout byte-identical to the lnxbuf run -> the lnxbuf run would reproduce R20-pq-2k4-ps100-cf2-s1; not queued; asked the coordinator whether to build an RTT-echo fix (+ lnxbuf caps) instead.
- runs/run_lever.sh now takes an optional runs/shadowbin-NAME (Shadow bin dir) and writes runs/NAME/shadow-bin.txt (replaced atomically 21:22; L-pq-b16-2k4-ps100-cf2-s1 shows the default path). Report: TCP caveat rewritten (RTO never adapts; buffers already Linux-like), Pending notes lnxbuf not queued. calib for the seed-1 PQ baseline: .amp-out/calib-R20-pq-2k4-ps100-cf2-s1.txt (att dup 2.28, block dup 3.62, att after 4 s p95 2.15 s).

The echo rule does not change the estimator at the timeouts.
- Shadow 3.3.0 FIFO qdisc (default interface_qdisc, configuration.rs L568): packets with sequence 0 (pure ACKs) get priority 0 (tcp.c L919-925), and the interface sends from the socket whose next packet has the lowest priority (interface.rs L94-99, a min-heap): pure ACKs never wait behind a host's upload backlog. A home router with a FIFO uplink would hold them. Likely why the echo rule changes little; added to the report's remaining TCP gaps, nothing changed.

### Coordinator 08:5x Nov 10: L seed 2 on rtt4; rtt5 sensitivity build
- Queue: L-pq-2k4-rtt4-ps100-cf2-s2 then L-pq-b16-2k4-rtt4-ps100-cf2-s2 (rtt4-log, nofallback) replace the current-build L seed-2 lines (commented). Order after R20-nopq-rtt4: fs0-rtt4 -> rtt5 pair (if it qualifies) -> L-pq-2k4-rtt4 -> fs0-b16-rtt4 (conditional) -> L-pq-2k4-rtt4-s2 -> L-pq-b16-rtt4-s2.
- rtt5 = rtt4 + Linux window validation (RTT5_CWV) + restart after idle (RTT5_SSR), both default on: .amp-out/rtt5_patch.py on an rsync copy of shadow-src-rtt4 (without build/) -> shadow-src-rtt5; clean build 132 s -> shadow-install-rtt5; wrapper shadow-install-rtt5-log/bin; patch repo/sim/shadow-3.3-rtt5.patch (6 files incl. tcp.h, relative to shadow-src, replaces rtt4.patch; round-trip checked). Gate in Reno's new-ACK dispatcher (slow start: cwnd < 2 x max_packets_out or last window cwnd-limited; CA: last window cwnd-limited; fast recovery not gated; its exit raises cwnd only under the CA rule); tcp_cwnd_validate bookkeeping at the end of _tcp_flush (packets out in sequence space = highestSequence + 1 - unacked); restart in _tcp_flush before the window check of a new data segment with nothing in flight, idle from info.lastDataSent (new and retransmitted data), RTO = retransmit.timeout. RFC 2861 slow part (tcp_cwnd_application_limited) not included.
- T64 validation started 08:58: runs/T64-rtt-rtt5 (on), runs/T64-rtt-rtt5off (RTT5_CWV=0 RTT5_SSR=0); scratch/t64-analyse5.sh. Decision rule: block p99 from publish (calib.py, now p50/p95/p99) and PQ p99 latency both within 10 % of T64-rtt-rtt4 -> no large runs; else R20-pq-2k4-rtt5 + R20-nopq-rtt5 after fs0-rtt4 (or after L-pq-2k4-rtt4 if not ready). T64-rtt-rtt4 cwnd over the run p50/p90: 1G->1G 161/16,967, 1G->home 52/484, home->1G 51/454, home->home 33/262.
- Finding (not tested): Shadow's Reno resets duplicate_ack_n only in slow start, fast-recovery exit and timeouts, not in congestion avoidance (ca_reno_cong_avoid_new_ack_ev_), so three non-consecutive duplicate ACKs trigger fast recovery; Linux counts consecutive duplicates.
- scratch/chains-nopq-rtt4.sh waits for the R20-nopq-rtt4 post line (calib, attmiss, bgexcess, pqdiag x3 + TCP chain).

- T64 runs on the rtt5 binary (shadow-install-rtt5; nothing queued on them; columns of the T64 wbsim table, reported with the R20-nopq-rtt5 report): runs/T64-rtt-rtt5cwv (RTT5_CWV=1 RTT5_SSR=0 RTT5_DUP=1: window validation only = Linux with net.ipv4.tcp_slow_start_after_idle=0, which also skips tcp_cwnd_application_limited) and runs/T64-rtt-rtt5ssr (RTT5_CWV=0 RTT5_SSR=1 RTT5_DUP=1: restart after idle only), started 19:41 alongside the queue (scratch/t64-run5cs.sh, log t64-run5cs.log 'all done'; outputs pqsum-/pqdiag-T64-rtt-NAME, tcpdiag-/rtt3diag-/tcppath-T64-NAME, calib-T64-rtt5cs.txt, blockslot-T64-rtt5cs.txt).
- Source check: Linux v6.12 net/ipv4/tcp_output.c (kernel.org stable tree, /tmp/linuxsrc-dl/tcp_output.c): tcp_cwnd_validate (L1913-1947) tracks is_cwnd_limited / max_packets_out / cwnd_usage_seq; when not cwnd-limited it keeps snd_cwnd_used = max packets_out and, if sysctl_tcp_slow_start_after_idle and now - snd_cwnd_stamp >= RTO and the congestion module has no cong_control hook (Reno, CUBIC), calls tcp_cwnd_application_limited (L1895-1911): in TCP_CA_Open and without SOCK_NOSPACE, win_used = max(snd_cwnd_used, initial cwnd); if win_used < cwnd: ssthresh = tcp_current_ssthresh, cwnd = (cwnd + win_used) >> 1; then snd_cwnd_used = 0 and snd_cwnd_stamp = now, so it repeats every RTO; when cwnd-limited it resets snd_cwnd_used and the stamp. rtt5 (shadow-src-rtt5 tcp.c L1594-1602 tcp_rtt5MayRaiseCwnd, L1756-1768 the tcp_cwnd_validate bookkeeping) has only the first part: no snd_cwnd_used, no cwnd stamp, no reduction. Added to the report's TCP gaps (rtt4 gaps bullet) and the rtt5 section's reading; README says rtt5 has the growth rule only. Build unchanged (coordinator). tcp_current_ssthresh (include/net/tcp.h L1343-1353, v6.12, /tmp/linuxsrc-dl/tcp.h): ssthresh outside cwnd reduction = max(ssthresh, cwnd/2 + cwnd/4), i.e. about 3/4 cwnd; cited in the report.

'queue' line 01:23 in realistic.log. Check pq_rate from run.json with python at start.
- (2) rtt6 = rtt5 plus Linux v6.12 CUBIC (net/ipv4/tcp_cubic.c), behind switches; Shadow 3.3.0 has only Reno. Port: bictcp_update with TCP friendliness and the cnt >= 2 cap; recalc_ssthresh with beta 717 and fast convergence; epoch shift on CA_EVENT_TX_START (data out with nothing in flight, tcp_event_data_sent); reset on entering Loss (not reversed by the undo); delay_min from RTT samples; keep RTT5_CWV's gating. HyStart (ack train + delay, v6.12 defaults) behind its own switch, on by default; report how often each test fires by path type.
  - T64 check, same config and seed: switches off byte-identical to rtt5; switches on: ssthresh = 0.7 x cwnd at each loss, cwnd follows W_max + 0.4 (t - K)^3 in a few traces, growth <= 1.5x per round trip, epoch shifts whenever sending resumes with nothing in flight, no stalls, RTO formula; by path type the share of data packets sent with cwnd >= ssthresh on rtt5 and rtt6; T64 wbsim table with rtt6 (HyStart on and off).
  - Rule: if block p99 from publish or PQ p99 in any class moves >= 10 % from rtt5 (HyStart on), queue R20-pq-b16-2k4-rtt6 then R20-pq-2k4-rtt6 right after b16-rtt5b, tcpd logging; if the bundled one passes the strict rule, queue L-pq-b16-2k4-rtt6 after them. Otherwise nothing on rtt6. Budget ~2.5 h from ~01:25 (to ~04:00). Short note to the coordinator when the check is done.

### rtt6 build (coordinator 01:1x Nov 11)
- 02:02 built shadow-install-rtt6 from shadow-src-rtt6 (rsync copy of shadow-src-rtt5b without build/ + python3 -I .amp-out/rtt6_patch.py; clean build 132 s, script .amp-out/scratch/build-rtt6.sh, log build-rtt6.log; 54 compiler warnings as rtt5b, none in the new code). -log wrapper shadow-install-rtt6-log/bin (LNXBUF_LOG=1).
- Port (all in tcp.c, Reno calls in at three points): CUBIC state in tcp->cubic (outside Reno's state, so the Eifel undo leaves the Loss reset in place, as Linux's tcp_reno_undo_cwnd); ssthresh_reduce -> cubictcp_recalc_ssthresh (epoch_start = 0, W_max with fast convergence, max(cwnd*717/1024, 2)) at fast-recovery entry and every RTO; Reno's CA handler -> bictcp_update (integer port incl. cubic_root, friendliness with beta_scale 15, cnt >= 2, 5 %/RTT cap while W_max = 0) + tcp_cong_avoid_ai on Reno's ACK counter (RTT5_CWV gate unchanged); every RTO -> bictcp_reset + hystart_reset (Linux's Loss state); TX_START in _tcp_flush when new data goes out with nothing outstanding (epoch += now - lastDataSent, capped at now); pkts_acked before Reno's new-ACK event with the estimator's sample (now - TSecr), skipping the ACK of the SYN and samples in the first second of an epoch; HyStart ack train (threshold (delay_min + ack_delay)/2, ack_delay from Linux's pacing-rate formula, no pacing) and delay (8 samples, clamp(delay_min/8, 4, 16 ms)); HyStart exit sets ssthresh = cwnd and switches Reno to CA at once (tcp_cong_reno_hystart_exit); cubictcp_init at ESTABLISHED. HZ = 1000.
- Diagnostics: snapshot fields txfl/txca (data packets released, and with cwnd >= ssthresh; all builds), cub, hstrain, hsdelay, epochs, txstart, txshift, rounds, rndover, rndoveracked, rndmax, dmin, lastmax, found; lines 'tcpd hys' (each HyStart exit), 'tcpd cubep' (each epoch start), and for ~12 traced endpoints at T64 'tcpd cubtr' (each full bictcp_update), 'tcpd cubrnd', 'tcpd txs'. Analysis .amp-out/rtt6diag.py RUNDIR [CONFIGDIR].
- 02:07 smoke test (T64 config, stop 45 s, 4 threads; deleted): no errors besides the expected 'StoppedByShadow' at the shortened stop; 2,559 epochs, K = cbrt((W_max - cwnd)/0.4) within 0.14 % (cubic_root); HyStart 194 train / 145 delay exits by sim 45 s. Trace selection hash was degenerate (no traces): fixed (fmix32 of host IP ^ peer IP x golden ratio; ~12 endpoints at T64), rebuilt 02:09 (127 s). repo/sim/shadow-3.3-rtt6.patch exported 02:1x (6 files, diff -u against shadow-src, round trip ok); README entry added.
- T64 chain .amp-out/scratch/t64-run6.sh (log t64-run6.log): waits for cwv's post line (cwv's peak PSS plus three T64 runs would pass 235 GiB), then runs rtt6off (RTT6_CUBIC=0 RTT6_HYSTART=0; byte identity against rtt5d), rtt6 (defaults) and rtt6nohs (RTT6_HYSTART=0) in parallel, then cmp, realism, pqsum, pqdiag, tcpdiag, rtt3diag, tcppath, rtt6diag, betacheck (--beta 0 on rtt6off, 1 on the others), calib-T64-rtt6.txt, blockslot-T64-rtt6.txt, gzip. t64table.py now also reads calib-T64-rtt6.txt and labels rtt6off / rtt6 / rtt6nohs.

### Coordinator 03:5x Nov 11: build rule for the remaining runs (applied 03:5x-04:1x Nov 11)
- Rule: each run after the rtt6 cells uses rtt6 if the rtt6 cell with the same bundling passes the strict rule, otherwise cwv. fs0-b16 and L-pq-b16 seed 2 follow R20-pq-b16-2k4-rtt6; L-pq-2k4 seeds 1 and 2 follow R20-pq-2k4-rtt6. The verdict rests on stock Linux wherever it passes, and on the sysctl only where stock fails.
- Order after the rtt6 cells (and L-pq-b16-2k4-rtt6 if it qualifies): fs0-b16 (its build is known ~09:00), L-pq-2k4 seed 1, L-pq-2k4 seed 2, L-pq-b16 seed 2. If either rtt6 cell passes, R20-nopq-rtt6 is added last, with the calibration table as for rtt5 (mainnet, rtt4, rtt5, rtt6 columns) and the matched BLS baseline.
- Reports: each rtt6 cell as soon as analysed, next to the same cell on rtt5 (and cwv for the unbundled one), with the per-slot table (1 Gbit completeness, 1 Gbit PQ missing, block p95 from publish, ssthresh p50 after restarts by path type), HyStart exits by path type and the cwnd >= ssthresh share.
- Done: runs/queue-night.txt: R20-pq-fs0-b16-2k4-cwv-ps100-cf2-s1 moved ahead of L-pq-2k4-cwv-ps100-cf2-s1 (old line commented, 'queue' line in realistic.log). .amp-out/scratch/decide-rtt6.sh b16 and pq (started 03:51; logs decide-rtt6-b16.log, -pq.log) wait for each cell's post line and pqdiag pass-rule line; on PASS they run .amp-out/scratch/queue_rtt6.py under flock runs/queue-night.txt.lock (b16: insert L-pq-b16-2k4-rtt6-ps100-cf2-s1 after R20-pq-2k4-rtt6, rename fs0-b16 and L-pq-b16 seed 2 to rtt6; pq: rename L-pq-2k4 seeds 1 and 2; both: append R20-nopq-rtt6-ps100-cf2-s1 if absent; shadowbin/nofallback written before the queue) and start chains-L.sh for the inserted L run and chains-nopqrtt6.sh for the nopq run; on FAIL or timeout they only log. ### Correction 13:5x Nov 11: late.py's "first after the deadline" counted stale copies
- Coordinator (13:2x) asked what late.py's first-delivery count includes, since it exceeded the misses at 8 s (L-pq-2k4 slot 40: 6 per 1 Gbit node, against 6.6 missing per node over all of slots 40-49).
- Cause: old late.py took the topic_msg_recv_counts delta over 8-16 s, averaged over all hosts of a class (%.0f). That counter also counts PQ copies older than max_msg_age 60 s: they pass gossipsub's 60 s duplicate_cache_time (wbsim main.rs L1477), count as new, and the client then drops them as stale_pq (n1432 slot 12 of R20-pq-2k4-ps100-cf2-s1: 308 counted in 8-16 s, all 308 stale_pq). When the 8 s tick was logged ~1 ms late it took the 7.5 s tick. Offline hosts were included (little effect).
- Fix: .amp-out/late.py now counts needed messages of the slot (own left out) first received after 8 s and by 16 s, from rx records, on online node-slots (pqdiag's rule), every slot, all three classes; also 'later' (> 16 s), 'stale' (stale_pq delta), copies, kB, iwant, mcache-miss; ticks matched within 50 ms. lslots.py parses it (column 'late first PQ deliveries (8-16 s), 1 Gbit host mean', .2f). Old outputs in scratch/late-old/; all 20 runs rerun (scratch/late-rerun.sh, rc 0); comparison scratch/latesum.py -> scratch/latesum-all.txt. In the 15 runs with per-slot pqdiag misses, no slot has first > misses.

- Registry: | L-pq-2k4-rtt6-ps100-cf2-s1 | 1,024 x 50 | yes, 2,458 B | x1.0 | cf2, Shadow rtt6 (log) | stopped (dropped, bundling adopted) 00:49 Nov 12 at sim 368 s (slots 0-27), 112.9 GiB PSS; not analysed |
- Answers sent 00:5x (coordinator asked where 2,000 per slot at 800k comes from and how a staker's subnet is chosen): 2,000 is an input (gen_config L112 --msgs default 2000; run_lever.sh L12 passes it); '800k' is the label from the earlier subnet study (sim/README.md L3); v600/v400 scale --msgs linearly with --consolidate (implies 1 per 400 validators per slot, stated nowhere). Signers: gen_config L292-300 + L337-339, 2,000 draws per slot with replacement, host weight = its validator count from data/f9ba162f-nodes.csv (126,255 validators on 1,639 of 2,048 hosts), so Binomial(2000, v/126,255) per host per slot, each sample validator signs with p ~1.6 %/slot (~6x the 1-in-400 the label implies); neither consolidation (except v600/v400) nor a committee. b16-rtt6 schedule: stakers publish 43-66 of ~2,000 signatures per slot (mean 48.2, 2.4 %); a staker publishes nothing in 95.2 % of its slots, 1 in 4.7 %, >= 2 in 0.1 %. Subnet: L345-346, every host not on 1 Gbit gets 1 + randrange(16) once per run; all its signatures go there (checked: every staker signature in the b16-rtt6 schedule is on its home subnet; --batch groups per (publisher, subnet), L365-370); non-stakers get a random one too; 1 Gbit hosts join all 16 and send each signature to a random subnet.
- gen_config.py: --staker-subnets N (with --one-subnet: each home staker joins home + N-1 random others; hosts without validators keep one) and --backbone-subnets N (every host not on 1 Gbit joins N random subnets; a staker also its home (duty) subnet and gets node field need=[home]), both from r3 = Random(seed * 6151 + 29), so no r2 draw changes. Generator check at 2,048 hosts (.amp-out/scratch/gen2048/{off,c1,c2}): off = R20-pq-b16-2k4-rtt6's run.json nodes, sched, bg and all 2,048 pubs byte for byte; c1 and c2 equal off apart from PQ topics and need (background topics unchanged; pq_rate 66.928125). c1: stakers 2 subnets (976), others as before, PQ subscriptions 13,178 -> 14,154, 801-838 -> 861-903 subscribers per subnet. c2: 843 stakers in 3, 133 in 2, 330 non-stakers in 2, 15,327 subscriptions, 938-985 per subnet; rule checked on 2,048 of 2,048 nodes.

# PQ Gasper one-subnet study (thread T-01a1e6e1-5dc7-7198-8015-4c45f7df4928, from Nov 17 01:2x)
Question: one PQ subnet (of 16) carrying 1,600 signatures per subnet per slot (800k / 32 / 16), every node joins it and
must hold all 1,600 at 8.0 s; signatures 2,404 B (leanXMSS), bundles of up to 16 per publisher, published 4.0-4.5 s.
Report: repo/results/pq-gasper-one-subnet.md. Analysis: .amp-out/pq1600.py RUN --per-slot (classes, per-signature
misses, completion times, late first deliveries, upload/download 4-8 s, dropped PQ forwards, PQ mesh makeup).
- Generator (repo/sim/gen_config.py, perturb.py): --single-subnet N (all signatures on topic 1, every node subscribes;
  scored as one of N PQ subnets: weight 1/N, P3 rate = mean messages per slot on the topic). Needed because
  --sig-subnets 1 uses topic 0, which wbsim's eth_peer_score never covers (it scores xmss_1..xmss_N). --no-att (perturb):
  no BLS attestation messages or subscriptions, random draws kept (other bg byte-identical, checked at 2,048 x 20).
  Old flag sets: run.json/sched/bg/pubs/shadow.yaml identical to the pre-change generator (256 x 3 check).
- Runner runs/run_pq1600.sh (ONE flags = night.sh REAL with --single-subnet 16 instead of --sig-subnets 16 --one-subnet,
  --no-att, --peer-scale 1.0, --msgs 1600 --msg-bytes 2404 --batch 16, cf2 binary and flags, seed 1); Shadow rtt6 without
  tcpd logging (shadow-install-rtt6/bin) via runs/shadowbin-NAME; ssr0 = shadow-install-rtt6-ssr0 (RTT5_SSR=0).
  Queue runs/pq1600-queue.sh + runs/queue-pq1600.txt (post: pq1600.py, pqdiag.py --per-slot, late.py into .amp-out/*-NAME.txt).
  Watchdog runs/watchdog256.sh 235 30, runs/memlog.sh, half-budget guard runs/pq1600-guard.py (other human user > 4 cores
  or > 16 GB for 10 min: queue paused, CPUs 72-95, stop the run if our PSS > 117 GiB; resumes after 10 quiet min).
- 2,048-host generation (seed 1): 742 1 Gbit / 976 50x25 / 330 50x15; 1,596-1,600 signatures per slot (rest lost to churn),
  301.8 messages per slot (bundle sizes 1: 2,370 ... 16: 941 of 6,036), 3.84 MB per slot; 97.6 % of signatures from 1 Gbit
  hosts. P3 on topic 1: weight 1/16, rate 301.8, threshold 73.5 at 64 hosts (rate 134.7) / ~165 at 2,048; LH full deficit
  -21.1 at 64 hosts; Teku/Lodestar/Nimbus -157.5 (max_positive still counts the 64 attestation subnets' weight 1: a PQ
  Gasper client would use 107.5; both prune at < 0).
- P1-T64-smoke (64 x 6, 8 threads, wall 300 s, maxrss 0.65 GB): slots 2-5 50/15 19.4 % complete (11-33 % per slot), sig
  miss 10.1 % at 8 s; 50/25 55.0 % (44-66 %), 4.9 %; 1 Gbit 100 %. Upload handed to TCP 4-8 s: 50/15 122 %, 50/25 84 %,
  1 Gbit 5 % of cap; download <= 45 %. PQ sends dropped after 1 s in the queue ~50 % in every class (incl. 1 Gbit).
  forward_messages_dropped_per_topic = timedout_messages_dropped_per_topic (the behaviour counts a forward timeout in both).
- Q2048x20-m1600-s1 started 01:42 Nov 17 (46 threads). Memory PSS: sim 35 76.8, 38 105.4, 46 116.4, 51 135.2, 62 149.5,
  74 162.3 GiB: per-slot burst increments +18 / +14 / +11 GiB (slots 1-3) -> projected peak 200-220 GiB. Speed ~5.3 min
  wall per slot after warm-up. Slots 0-1 (partial pq1600.py): 50/15 18.2 / 37.0 %, 50/25 50.1 / 53.4 %, 1 Gbit 97.3 / 98.0 %.
- 02:1x first message to the coordinator (setup, plan, memory, ETA ~20:00 CET). Queue order: m400, m200 (added: the first
  study's one-topic bundled cell failed 50/15 at 1.2 MB per slot), m800, fs75, clean, ssr0, 512 hosts.
- 02:2x coordinator asks for the findings (no plan change): (1) what holds back delivery to home nodes although their
  downloads stay < 50 % (own saturated uplink delaying IWANTs / TCP ACKs, or saturated mesh peers dropping forwards),
  with evidence; (2) report the 800 run as approximating 1,600 signatures of ~1.2 kB (Potuz), and whether the
  different message count could change the result.
  Evidence so far: 50/15 vs 50/25 (same 50 Mbit/s down, same peer population) miss 11.4 vs 4.4 % of signatures in slots
  0-1 at 2,048 hosts; 1 Gbit hosts drop ~48 % of PQ forwards after 1 s in a peer queue at ~5 % uplink use; more 1 Gbit
  peers in a home node's PQ mesh did not help (complete vs incomplete node-slots 34.7 vs 40.6 % 1 Gbit share, 50/15).
  Added Q512x8-m1600-leaf30-s1 (512 x 8, --freeride-share 0.3: 30 % of all hosts never forward; tcpd logging via
  shadow-install-rtt6-log) right after the headline: leaf vs forwarding home nodes in the same run (pq1600.py splits
  '-leaf' classes), and .amp-out/leafdiag.py (RTOs and spurious RTOs per 1,000 data packets by path type, leaves split
  out; spurious = ACK of the original arrived after the timeout: a delay spike, on paths into a home node mostly its
  ACKs behind its own upload). runs/run_pq1600.sh: --with-att keeps BLS attestations (edited while the headline's
  instance ran; the leftover lines it reads afterwards rewrite the same shadowbin file and re-call run_lever.sh, which
  exits on the existing run dir: harmless).
- Report skeleton repo/results/pq-gasper-one-subnet.md (question, pass rule, setup, caveats, commands). Schedule:
  check-in every 2 h from 04:15 CET (set_schedule, existing mode).
- Memory Q2048x20-m1600-s1: sim 88 170.6 GiB (slot-4 burst +8.2): increments +18/+14/+11/+8 -> projected peak ~195-205.
- 02:2x headline memory: slot increments +18/+14/+11/+8.2/+9.4/+6.8 GiB (188.6 at sim 110): may reach the 235 cap around
  slot 13-15. Added runs/pq1600-memresume.sh (PID 527040): after a watchdog mem stop (stop-run.sh moves the dir once the
  post line exists) it comments the run's queue line and removes STOP-night unless runs/HALF-pq1600 exists, so the queue
  goes on; completed slots stay in runs/stopped-NAME-mem (post-processing ran on them). Slots 2-4 (partial): 50/15
  35.2/33.6/26.1 %, 50/25 57.8/57.0/57.1 %, 1 Gbit 98.9/99.9/99.5 %; 50/15 miss 12.5 % of signatures at 8 s.

## Q512x8-m1600-leaf30-s1 (mechanism: 1,600 per slot, 512 x 8, --freeride-share 0.3 = 30 % of hosts never forward, tcpd logging via shadow-install-rtt6-log; 03:26-03:42 Nov 17, wall 972 s, peak PSS 33.5 GiB)
- Forwarding vs leaf (same run, slots 2-7): complete 50/15 24.86 vs 74.00 %, 50/25 52.74 vs 79.07 %, 1 Gbit 97.85 vs 97.67 %;
  signatures missing at 8 s 17.29 vs 2.24 %, 6.61 vs 2.01 %, 0.247 vs 0.109 %; at 9 s 12.65 vs 1.18, 4.54 vs 0.85, 0.175 vs 0.035 %.
- Upload 4-8 s: forwarding 105.5 / 88.4 / 6.1 %, leaves 1.8 / 1.3 / 0.2 %. Download 4-8 s: forwarding 31.3 / 39.9 / 2.5 %, leaves
  46.4 / 46.8 / 2.0 %. PQ sends dropped after 1 s: forwarding 36 / 46 / 38 %. Copies per needed message 3.09 / 3.26 / 3.55 vs leaves
  3.68 / 3.60 / 2.85; IWANT-requested 757 / 588 / 103 vs 622 / 556 / 178. Home-published signatures missed less in every class.
- 1 Gbit share of the PQ mesh, complete vs incomplete: 50/15 38.8 vs 39.8, 50/25 39.5 vs 45.5, 50/15 leaves 32.3 vs 33.5,
  50/25 leaves 28.0 vs 30.1 %: mesh makeup does not predict completeness.
- leafdiag (slots 2-7; per 1,000 data packets RTOs, retransmissions; SRTT minus configured RTT p50/p90/p99 ms):
  1G -> 50/15 12.90, 49.0, 44/179/441 vs 1G -> 50/15-leaf 0.41, 2.7, 7/11/24; 1G -> 50/25 6.70, 24.8, 12/49/143 vs leaf 0.41, 2.6,
  6/11/26; 50/25 -> 50/15 30.45, 49.3, 50/193/468 vs leaf 2.62, 6.4, 9/16/35; 50/15 -> 50/15 44.96, 56.8, 41/167/446; 1G -> 1G
  0.20, 0.41, 1/3/5. Out of forwarding homes into 1 Gbit 3.2-3.5 RTOs, 4-6 retx, p99 50-58 ms. Spurious share of RTOs into
  forwarding homes 29-32 %, into leaves 39-42 %.
- Reading: the forwarding duty saturates a home node's uplink; its TCP ACKs and IWANTs queue behind its own data, so paths into it
  see inflated RTTs and ~30x the RTOs of a leaf on the same link; senders' queues to it back up and drop forwards after 1 s (even
  1 Gbit senders drop 38-39 %), while its download stays at 31-40 %. Lighthouse-style slow-peer penalties then mark it negative
  and prune it from meshes. Leaves' remaining ~2 % (fail 99 % too): from saturated senders (their home mesh peers drop
  forwards; home -> leaf paths 2-3 RTOs per 1,000); this part is inferred, not split by sender.
- 800 vs 1,600 x 1.2 kB (coordinator ask 2), prepared 04:3x: Potuz (potuz.net/posts/pq, fetched with curl; web tool over its usage
  limit): 'Attestations will now come with 2KB or 1.2KB signatures' (1.2 kB = without native DVT support), '30K of them at the
  3-second mark'. wbsim verification is per signature (verify_cost_ns: 400 us x nsigs x cpu factor; 4 workers home, 8 cloud;
  232 of 1,306 home hosts at cpu 3.0), so 1,600 x 1.2 kB verifies twice m800's signatures. Headline split by CPU (SPLITCPU=1,
  .amp-out/pq1600cpu-Q2048x20-m1600-s1.txt): 50/15 slow CPU 25.9 % complete / 14.6 % signatures missing vs 29.0 / 12.4 %;
  50/25 slow 59.5 / 3.8 vs 55.0 / 4.2: 3x verification time makes no consistent difference, so doubling verifications should
  not either. Messages per slot: 1.2 kB option 301.8 (half-size bundles), m800 198.5. To do at the m800 check-in: control and
  other non-PQ-data upload in 4-8 s per message (tx minus PQ topic bytes) at 1,600/800/400, to size what +103 messages adds.
- 04:3x report updated (verdict so far, overview table, headline table, leaf/TCP mechanism section). m400 sim 166, PSS 131 GiB.

  identical across loads, so the load-to-load difference is PQ control + framing) and PQ bytes per received copy (= mean message
  size, so topic_msg_recv_bytes counts every copy). 50/15 / 50/25 / 1 Gbit: 200 (90.4 msgs/slot) 398 / 411 / 496; 400 (136.7)
  569 / 567 / 654; 1,600 (301.8) 1,079 / 901 / 930. 200 -> 400: +3.7 / +3.4 / +3.4 kB per extra message per node (IHAVE to
  ~0.25 x non-mesh peers for 3 heartbeats, IDONTWANT, IWANT, framing); 400 -> 1,600: +3.1 / +2.0 / +1.7 (saturated homes receive
  less of it). Outputs for m200, m400, m1600 regenerated.

  its access link, 13 ms one-way, 2.5 ms jitter; fiber 0.05 %, 4 ms, 0.5; cable 0.1 %, 8 ms, 1.5), full realism:
  300: 50/15 100 / 99.95 / 99.14 (DSL 11 of 12 incomplete node-slots), 50/25 100 / 99.98 / 99.71 (11 of 12).
  400: 50/15 99.96 / 99.52 / 97.65 (1 / 10 / 30 of 41; signatures missing 0 / 0.026 / 0.171 %; upload 4-8 s 59 / 56 / 50 %),
       50/25 99.95 / 99.83 / 98.31 (4 / 10 / 63 of 77).
  600: 50/15 97.39 / 95.79 / 85.21, 50/25 99.68 / 98.63 / 88.81. 800: 50/15 90.26 / 84.58 / 66.67, 50/25 98.13 / 94.93 / 75.92.
  1,600: 50/15 31.75 / 25.53 / 26.87 (upload 123 / 115 / 99 %), 50/25 62.29 / 55.58 / 42.20.
  400 clean: 50/15 99.88 / 100 / 100, 50/25 100 / 100 / 100. 400 ssr0: 50/15 100 / 99.95 / 98.51 (DSL 19 of 20), 50/25 100 / 99.97 /
  98.93 (40 of 42).
- NAT at 400: 50/15 NAT 99.38 vs 99.28 % complete, 50/25 99.69 vs 99.50: no effect. Slow CPU (x3 verification) vs rest, 50/15:
  300 99.70 vs 99.82, 400 98.79 vs 99.41, 800 82.53 vs 83.31, 1,600 25.87 vs 29.04; 50/25 99.94 vs 99.93, 99.40 vs 99.60, 92.91 vs
  92.21, 59.52 vs 54.97: small for 50/15, none for 50/25.
- Reading: at 300-400, packet loss on DSL access links decides which home nodes miss (DSL hosts hand less to TCP than fiber hosts
  yet hold 73-92 % of the incomplete node-slots; the clean run keeps DSL latency and jitter and drops loss, churn, NAT and slow CPUs,
  and its DSL hosts are all complete; NAT no effect, slow CPU small). Fiber home hosts fall to 97.4 % (50/15) at 600 and 98.1 %
  (50/25) at 800, so without the perturbations the boundaries would likely lie at 400-600 (50/15) and 600-800 (50/25): inferred,
  not run. At 1,600 every access type fails by far (uplink-bound).

- Router drops: Shadow logs RouterDropped only at trace level (packet.rs add_status), compiled out (release_max_level_debug),
  so no direct count without a new build. Instead the --pcap captures: eth0.pcap records sent packets at interface pop
  (before the path's loss) and received packets at interface push (after the inbound CoDel queue and the download rate
  limit; host.rs relay_inet_in, interface.rs L259-L265); Shadow's TCP numbers data packets one by one (tcp.c L1518).
- 16:2x diagnostic tools: .amp-out/downqtcp.py (leafdiag.py for any link classes; checked on T64-2sub: every row identical
  to leafdiag.py's) and .amp-out/pcapdrop.py (from --pcap captures: per captured sender/receiver pair and TCP sequence
  number, transmissions on the sender's wire vs copies at the receiver's interface = path loss + inbound CoDel drops,
  against the configured path loss from the GML (Shadow's shortest path by latency); lower bound for all senders into the
  captured home hosts from sequence holes; download use per 10 ms). Checked on S64x6-h1000x20-smoke (home 1000/20, normal
  loss, so no downlink queueing): lost per 1,000 home->home 4.21 vs 3.50 configured (11 of 2,613), 1G->home 0.74 vs 1.04
  (6 of 8,097), home->1G 1.34 vs 1.04 (12 of 8,946): equal within noise. pcap timestamps are simulation time (PQ data at
  4.5-6 s after first_slot_s + n x 12, aggregates at 8.5-9.5 s). .amp-out/scratch/diag512-chain.sh (started 16:2x,
  detached) waits for each S512x8 run's post line and writes .amp-out/downqtcp-NAME.txt and pcapdrop-NAME.txt (DIAG line).
